These are free in the sense that no proof got longer; several are free in the
stronger sense that no proof changed at all.  The right-hand column is the one
to read sceptically.

\medskip
\noindent
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.20\textwidth}p{0.22\textwidth}p{0.24\textwidth}p{0.26\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Axis} & \textbf{Sources} & \textbf{Here} & \textbf{Used by} \\
\midrule
Time index
  & $\T = \Rp$
  & \eqref{T0}--\eqref{T4}, Definition~\ref{def:bundles}
  & the poset case really survives in Section~\ref{sec:MP}, in
    Theorem~\ref{thm:absuniq}\ref{it:absuniq_a}, in
    Theorem~\ref{thm:absconv} and in the kernel layer of
    Section~\ref{ssec:exduality} \\
Compensator
  & $\int_0^t g(X_s)\dif s$
  & a \emph{clock}, Definition~\ref{def:clock}
  & discrete time (Corollary~\ref{cor:dualdiscrete},
    Remark~\ref{rem:jumpdiscrete}) and fixed times of discontinuity
    (Section~\ref{ssec:weakstrong}) become instances, not analogues \\
Convention $\iota$
  & invisible, the clock being atomless
  & both carried
  & Section~\ref{sec:cadlag} needs $\mathrm{o}$;
    Example~\ref{ex:invariance} needs $\mathrm{o}$ after embedding and
    $\mathrm{p}$ before it (Remark~\ref{rem:embedflip}) \\
State space
  & Polish
  & \eqref{E0}--\eqref{E3}, Definition~\ref{def:Ebundles}
  & martingale problems for $\mathcal{S}'(\R^d)$-valued processes are covered by
    Sections~\ref{sec:MP}--\ref{sec:duality} with no new proof
    (Remark~\ref{rem:E1why}) \\
State space, fibred
  & fixed $E$
  & $E_t$ varying, Definition~\ref{def:Efibred}
  & forced by the historical process of Section~\ref{ssec:exduality}; buys the
    space-time martingale problem \eqref{eq:sectiontest} \\
Scalar field
  & $\R$
  & $\K \in \{\R,\C\}$
  & the form the transform literature uses; Theorem~\ref{thm:duality} needs no
    change at all (Remark~\ref{rem:complexvalued}) \\
Local problems
  & a variant
  & available from Definition~\ref{def:absMP} on
  & primary, not optional, for path-dependent rates
    (Remark~\ref{rem:pathjumpprimary}) \\
Path space
  & $\DE$ with $J_1$, Polish
  & any measurable $F$; \eqref{eq:C1prime}
  & Remark~\ref{rem:absconvtopfree}: \ref{it:C1} and \ref{it:C3a} are one
    hypothesis about finitely many real random variables, so
    Theorem~\ref{thm:absconv} is topology-free and
    Theorem~\ref{thm:MZconv} follows \\
Mode of convergence
  & $J_1$
  & also convergence in measure
  & Section~\ref{ssec:measureconv}; \MZ{} tightness needs no compact
    containment \\
Approximating operators
  & $\lVert f_n - f\rVert \to 0$
  & \eqref{eq:approxpath}, along the paths
  & Corollary~\ref{cor:EKconvpath}: locally uniform convergence suffices under
    \eqref{eq:ccn}, which is assumed anyway \\
Existence from a dual
  & \DGP: $\Rp$, Lebesgue, $E_1$ compact
  & any shift invariant clock, \eqref{E1}, fibred
  & Section~\ref{ssec:exduality}; the compactness of \DGP{} survives only in
    Proposition~\ref{prop:rieszmarkov}, i.e.\ in \emph{checking} \ref{it:D3} \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}

\medskip
\noindent
Three of these deserve a sentence beyond the table.

\emph{The index bundles pay for themselves by what they exclude.}  Separating
\eqref{T2a} from \eqref{T2b} makes visible that Section~\ref{sec:cadlag} needs a topology on
$\T$ and Sections~\ref{sec:uniqueness} and \ref{sec:duality} need an algebraic
property, which $\Rp$ happens to supply at once; and it turns up the fact that
\eqref{T1p}, the metric lattice that \EK, Section~2.8 builds and that the first
draft of this manuscript took as the base assumption, is used \emph{nowhere}.

\emph{The two conventions are not bookkeeping.}  Remark~\ref{rem:embedflip} is
the sharpest evidence: the same Markov chain needs $\iota = \mathrm{p}$
intrinsically on $\T = \N_0$ and $\iota = \mathrm{o}$ once its grid is embedded
in $\Rp$, because with $\iota = \mathrm{p}$ an atom at $u$ is compensated
\emph{after} $u$ while $f(X)$ jumps \emph{at} $u$.  A manuscript committed to one
convention could not state Example~\ref{ex:invariance}.

\emph{Free is not the same as empty, but it is close to it in one case.}  The
complex scalar field changed no proof and no hypothesis; its value is entirely
that it lets Section~\ref{sec:duality} be applied in the form the affine and
affine Volterra literature actually uses.  Whether that counts as a
generalization or as a change of font is a matter of taste.

\subsection{Gaps and slips in the sources}
\label{ssec:addedcorrections}

\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{\EK, Chapter~3, Problem~7} is stated as an exercise and used in the
  last step of their Theorem~4.3.6.  Remark~\ref{rem:sepcondproof} proves it, and
  records that the countability the argument needs comes from the state space and
  not from the separating class.
\item \emph{\EK, Section~2.8} is easy to misread in three ways, and
  Remark~\ref{rem:ekindex} sets them out: the lattice is needed for $\vee$ and
  not for $\wedge$ (their Remark~8.3 warns that $\tau \wedge a$ need not be a
  stopping time); $\Filt_\tau$ needs no infima; and the standing lattice
  hypothesis is declared before a series of definitions none of which uses it.
\item \emph{\EK, (3.4)} quantifies the finite-dimensional criterion over chains
  $t_1 \leq \dots \leq t_n \leq s$.  On a preordered index that is too weak, as
  Remark~\ref{rem:fddnochain} shows, and the proof of
  Proposition~\ref{prop:fddchar} needs arbitrary finite subsets of $\T_{\leq s}$ ---
  the times produced by the Fubini step cannot be chained.
\item \emph{Bounded pointwise closure} (\EK{} 4.3.1) is more than the argument
  uses.  Lemma~\ref{lem:closure} is dominated convergence, applies to unbounded
  $f,g$, and delivers the martingale property for the filtration at hand rather
  than only for \eqref{eq:starfilt}; Remark~\ref{rem:bpscope} isolates what the
  bp-apparatus is for.
\item \emph{Linear Hawkes processes do not explode}, whatever
  $\lVert\phi\rVert_1$: the Volterra resolvent exists locally for every locally
  integrable kernel.  What $\lVert\phi\rVert_1 = 1$ separates is stability, not
  existence (Example~\ref{ex:hawkes}).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Generalizations that turned out to be empty}
\label{ssec:addednothing}

Four things looked like gains and are not.  They are recorded because a
blueprint that lists only its successes is not usable as a map.

\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \emph{``Every process is Markov if the state is the path.''}  True, and
  vacuously so: $\sigma(\hat{X}_t) = \Filt^X_t$, an identity of $\sigma$-fields
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:liftmarkov}).  The one-dimensional distributions of the lift
  are the finite-dimensional distributions of the process, so every hypothesis of
  the Markov layer becomes its own conclusion.  A Markov structure is a
  \emph{compression} statement, and the lift attains it by deleting the
  compression; see Remark~\ref{rem:liftcollapse}.
\item \emph{``The abstract martingale problem is a special case of the Markovian
  one.''}  Formally yes, with $g = 0$ on the lift, and as empty as the observation
  that every process solves the martingale problem for the family of its own
  martingales.  The informative lift keeps the generator and lets its integrand be
  a path functional --- which is Setting~\ref{set:pathjump}, already in the
  manuscript under another name (Remark~\ref{rem:liftform}).
\item \emph{``Every clock admits duality.''}  Withdrawn.  What is true is
  Corollary~\ref{cor:atomless}, and it is a deterministic time change of the
  Lebesgue case rather than a theorem; the atomic case is
  Proposition~\ref{prop:atomicdual} and needs the atoms below a point to form a
  finite chain, and the mixed case is open
  (Remark~\ref{rem:atomsnotchange}).
\item \emph{``Submartingale problems are a further free generalization.''}  They
  are transverse, not wider: formulating a submartingale needs an order and
  therefore excludes $\K = \C$.  Over $\R$ they are a genuine generalization, by
  \eqref{eq:submartsym}, but the determining set does not transfer for free
  (Lemma~\ref{lem:semiring}) and uniqueness fails outright
  (Remark~\ref{rem:submartlost}).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent
Items (i) and (ii) share a moral worth stating once: enlarging the state space
never restricts the class of processes, so a construction that only enlarges it
cannot prove anything.  Item (iii) has a different one, and it is about method
rather than mathematics: it began as the repair of a genuine error --- the claim
that duality \emph{requires} a translation invariant clock --- and the repair
overshot, turning ``translation invariance is unnecessary'' into ``every clock
works'' and a substitution into a theorem.

\begin{remark}[Where the graduation stops, and why]
  \label{rem:graduation}
  Definition~\ref{def:bundles} graduates the hypotheses on $\T$, and
  Section~\ref{ssec:bundletable} records which result needs which bundle.  What
  neither says is where the graduation \emph{ends}.  It ends in four different
  places, for four different reasons, and a reader who tries to push past any of
  them should know in advance which wall is being hit.

  \medskip
  \noindent
  \begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.23\textwidth}p{0.30\textwidth}p{0.41\textwidth}@{}}
    \toprule
    \textbf{What is being defined} & \textbf{Weakest $\T$ that carries it}
      & \textbf{What breaks beyond it} \\
    \midrule
    Filtration, adaptedness, the martingale property
      & \eqref{T0}: a preorder with a least element
      & Nothing: there is no weaker structure in which
        $E[X_t \mid \Filt_s] = X_s$ for $s \leq t$ can be written.  Mathlib
        indexes \texttt{Filtration} and \texttt{Martingale} by
        \texttt{[Preorder }$\iota$\texttt{]} for the same reason \\[0.6ex]
    Stopping times closed under the operations of Fact~\ref{fact:stoppingtimes}
      & \eqref{T1}: a lattice, for $\vee$
      & $\max_{k \leq n} \tau_k$ ceases to be an element of $\T$.  It is $\vee$
        and not $\wedge$ that is needed, for the reason set out in
        Remark~\ref{rem:ekindex}(ii) \\[0.6ex]
    C\`adl\`ag paths
      & A \emph{tree order}: \eqref{T2a} relaxed to ``$\{s : s \leq t\}$ is a
        chain for every $t$'', with a countable dense subset as in \eqref{T2b}
      & The left limit stops being a limit.  On a merely directed index
        $\{s : s \leq t\}$ is not linearly ordered, so ``the limit from below''
        is a limit along a directed rather than a linearly ordered filter, and
        its existence is a hypothesis instead of a consequence \\[0.6ex]
    The Skorokhod $J_1$ topology
      & \eqref{T3}, refined: $\T$ a closed subset of $\Rp$ containing $0$, with
        the induced metric
      & The time changes lose control of the paths; see below \\
    \bottomrule
  \end{tabular}

  \medskip
  Rows three and four are the ones that are not obvious, and they fail
  differently.

  \emph{Row three} is the reason \eqref{T2a} appears where it does.  A tree
  order --- every down-set a chain --- is the exact generality in which
  the c\`adl\`ag condition of Definition~\ref{def:process} can be \emph{stated}: left limits are taken along
  $\{s : s \leq t\}$, and that is a chain.  This is not idle: the genealogy of a
  branching process is such an index, and the cluster recursion of
  Proposition~\ref{prop:hawkesduality} builds its process along one.  What a tree
  does \emph{not} have is $\vee$, so by row two its stopping times are not closed
  under $\max$; the branching literature replaces them by stopping lines.  The
  consequence for this manuscript is clean, because it follows the line
  Section~\ref{sec:formalization} already draws for other reasons: on a tree
  index, Sections~\ref{sec:MP}, \ref{ssec:absuniq}, \ref{sec:duality} and
  Theorem~\ref{thm:absconv} survive verbatim, and everything that invokes
  Fact~\ref{fact:optsampl} --- Theorem~\ref{thm:absstrongmarkov} and the local
  theory of Section~\ref{ssec:localmp} --- does not.

  \emph{Row four} fails for a reason of its own, and metrizing $\T$ does not
  repair it.  Billingsley's $\lVert \lambda \rVert^\circ$ is, for a time change
  fixing the endpoints, exactly
  \[
    \lVert \lambda \rVert^\circ
      \;=\; \sup_{s < t} \Bigl| \log \tfrac{\lambda t - \lambda s}{t - s} \Bigr|
      \;=\; \log \max\bigl( \operatorname{Lip}(\lambda),\, \operatorname{Lip}(\lambda^{-1}) \bigr),
  \]
  so it is defined on any metric $\T$, and the two facts the completeness proof
  uses --- $\lVert \lambda^{-1} \rVert^\circ = \lVert \lambda \rVert^\circ$ and
  $\lVert \lambda \circ \mu \rVert^\circ \leq \lVert \lambda \rVert^\circ +
  \lVert \mu \rVert^\circ$ --- become the two standard properties of Lipschitz
  constants.  What does \emph{not} come for free is the control
  $\sup_t \rho(\lambda t, t) \leq \psi(\lVert \lambda \rVert^\circ)$ with
  $\psi(0) = 0$, and without it the norm no longer sees whether $\lambda$ moves
  the paths.  That control holds when $\rho$ is additive along the order,
  $\rho(s,u) = \rho(s,t) + \rho(t,u)$ for $s \leq t \leq u$ --- and additivity
  makes $t \mapsto \rho(0,t)$ an order isomorphism and isometry onto a closed
  subset of $\Rp$.  Hence the entry in the table: not an interval, but not more
  than a closed subset either.  The class is larger than it looks --- it contains
  $h\N_0$, so Section~\ref{ssec:diffclocks} compares objects of one family rather
  than of two, and it contains the closed range of a subordinator, which is the
  time set of excursion theory.  A tree is not in it: a root-fixing isometry may
  permute two isomorphic branches, so $\lVert \lambda \rVert^\circ = 0$ while
  $\sup_t \rho(\lambda t, t)$ is of the order of the diameter.  Restricting to
  time changes that keep every point on its own branch restores the control, at
  the price of making the group depend on the tree.

  \emph{Manifolds.}  The natural question, given rows three and four, is whether
  a manifold can serve as $\T$, and the answer separates the two halves of
  c\`adl\`ag.  Existence of one-sided limits is a regularity condition;
  \emph{choosing the right-hand one} is a choice of a side, and in dimension
  $\geq 2$ the discontinuity set of a function that has such limits is generically
  a hypersurface, which has two sides only if it is co-orientable.  A jump across
  the core curve of a M\"obius band has no consistent right-hand side, and
  the c\`adl\`ag condition of Definition~\ref{def:process} cannot be stated for it.  Where the geometry
  cooperates the notion does exist and is classical: for $\T = [0,1]^d$ with the
  product order one asks for limits from all $2^d$ quadrants and continuity from
  one of them, and that space carries a Skorokhod topology and tightness criteria
  \cite{Neu71,BW71,Str72,Whitt02}; on a domain in $\R^d$ the $BV$ and $SBV$ theory
  supplies a $(d-1)$-rectifiable jump set with two traces across an oriented
  normal \cite{AFP00}, which is the same notion carried by geometric measure
  theory; and for an index ordered by inclusion there is the outer continuity
  with inner limits of \cite{IM00}.  Each of these fixes the missing datum by
  hand --- a coordinate frame, a co-orientation, a set algebra.  A manifold
  supplies none of them canonically, which is the precise sense in which
  c\`adl\`ag carries a one-dimensional structure that continuity does not.

  \emph{And the ceiling that is not there.}  None of this constrains
  Section~\ref{ssec:measureconv}.  The pseudo-path of Fact~\ref{fact:pseudopath}
  is the image of a finite measure on $\T$ under $u \mapsto (u, w(u))$; it needs
  a measure on $\T$ and a metric on $E$, and no order, no direction and no
  dimension.  Its Borel field is $\sigma(\pi_u)$, which is the cylinder field and
  presupposes nothing.  So Theorem~\ref{thm:absconv} in the reading of
  Remark~\ref{rem:absconvtopfree}, together with
  Section~\ref{ssec:measureconv}, has no ceiling of the kind tabulated above:
  the clock of Definition~\ref{def:clock} is the whole of what it uses.  That is
  the sharpest statement of what the abstraction of Section~\ref{ssec:recommendation}
  bought --- not that the results hold under weaker hypotheses on $\T$, but that
  the two that never used $\T$ can be seen not to.

  \emph{The one index in the table that is out of reach for a different reason.}
  A linearly ordered $\T$ with no countable dense subset --- an uncountable
  ordinal, the long line --- satisfies row three and fails \eqref{T2b}.  There
  c\`adl\`ag is definable and the theory is empty: on $\omega_1$ every continuous
  real function is eventually constant.  It belongs in this subsection on its
  own merits.
\end{remark}
